## Document 143: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-21
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다. 
Buy (Maintain)

본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

## Document 149: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields
Date:    4 hours ago
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears and Lowers Bond Yields

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Scare Raises Growth Fears
and Lowers Bond Yields
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 15 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jonny Gios / Unsplash

I. Treasury Yields
US Treasury yields fell sharply today, extending Wednesday's retreat from
intraday highs. The 10-year yield dropped 9bps to 5.10%, while the 30-
year fell 10bps to 5.43% (chart).

### Page 2

Even the 2-year yield declined slightly to 4.77% (chart).
Remarkably, this broad-based decline occurred despite two hotter-than-
expected inflation reports. Normally, such surprises would push Treasury
yields higher as investors priced in a more hawkish Fed.
So, what's going on? We think the bond market is focusing on the
sustainability of the AI investment boom. That issue came to the fore this
week as CoreWeave's bonds plunged below 80 cents on the dollar and

debt financing major data center projects also tumbled. A new Gartner
report estimates AI data center operators will need $8.5 trillion in revenues
through 2030 to cover costs and earn healthy returns, versus projected
revenues of "just" $3.6 trillion.

### Page 3

Why does this matter? The AI buildout has become a major engine of US
economic growth. But doubts about future returns are pushing up
borrowing costs, potentially constraining access to capital and ultimately
slowing investment spending.
As a result, investors may be lowering their economic growth expectations
amid concerns that the AI boom could slow, pushing Treasury yields lower.
This reinforces our longstanding view that the earlier surge in Treasury

yields largely reflected stronger-than-expected nominal GDP growth
(chart).
We are nowhere near ready to write off the AI investment boom. Demand
for compute continues to outpace supply, while rising adoption should fuel
further investment. Indeed, just five hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet,
Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle) are expected to spend a combined $892
billion this year and $1.1 trillion next year. Their strong balance sheets and
cash flow generation make their spending plans relatively insensitive to

higher borrowing costs. While heavily indebted AI infrastructure providers
may struggle, we expect the hyperscalers to keep spending and the
Roaring 2020s to keep roaring.
II. Inflation
Let's take a closer look at September's hotter-than-expected PPI report
and what it tells us about inflationary pressures in the pipeline.

### Page 4

(1) PPI for final demand. September's PPI final demand surged 1.3%
m/m, largely reflecting a 10.8% spike in energy prices. However, even
excluding the volatile food, energy, and trade services components,
producer prices still rose 0.5%, suggesting that inflationary pressures
remain broad-based.
On a y/y basis, core PPI final demand rose to 5.0%, with services and
goods prices rising 5.0% and 5.2%, respectively (chart).
(2) PPI for consumption. The PPI for personal consumption, a key input

into the Fed’s preferred PCED inflation gauge, surged by 1.3% m/m in
September (chart). Excluding food and energy, it was up 0.6%.

### Page 5

Core PPI personal consumption rose to 4.6% y/y and remained well
above both core PCED and core CPI inflation, suggesting that upside
risks to consumer inflation remain elevated (chart).
(3) Supercore inflation. The PPI supercore inflation rate rose to 4.6% y/y
in September (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Core PCED. With September CPI and PPI now in hand, most
forecasters expect core PCED inflation to rise 0.3% m/m in September,
which would lift the y/y rate from 3.0% to 3.1%. The Cleveland Fed’s
Inflation Nowcasting model projects 0.28% m/m and 3.05% y/y,
respectively.
III. Booming Economy
For now, the US economy shows no signs of slowing down. Consider the
latest data points:
(1) Jobless claims. Initial jobless claims edged up slightly to 199,000 in

the week ended October 10, the fifth straight week below 200,000. That is
the longest streak since 1969! Continuing claims declined to 1.71 million,
dragging down the 4-week moving average to the lowest level since April
2023 (chart).

### Page 7

(2) Retail sales. September retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, partly driven by a
6.4% surge in gasoline station sales amid elevated pump prices (chart).
Still, eight of the 13 major categories posted gains. Sales at food services
and drinking places, the only services category and a key discretionary
spending indicator, jumped 1.1% m/m, their eighth consecutive monthly
gain. They rose 7.0% y/y, the strongest increase since March 2023.
(3) GDP. Reflecting today's retail sales report, the Atlanta Fed’s GDPNow

model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at 3.6% (saar), unchanged from
October 8 (chart). Consumer spending is projected to rise 3.6%. AI-related
demand is boosting business equipment investment (17.6%), fixed
business structures (7.0%), and intellectual property investment (6.6%).
Final sales to private domestic purchasers are expected to grow 4.7%.

### Page 8

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh đư ợc 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi 
bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

## Document 203: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

## Document 231: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 7

## Document 241: First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #newzealand
First Impressions: NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion,
September quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
08:35 October 06 2026 Share
Businesses are feeling more optimistic about the months ahead, but current conditions
remain tough for many industries. Cost pressures eased off a little compared to last quarter.
Key results (seasonally adjusted)
General business situation: +40 (Prev: +14)

### Page 2

Trading activity, past three months: -1 (Prev: +1) 
Trading activity, next three months: +15 (Prev: +11) 
Average selling prices, past three months: +27 (Prev: +37) 
Average selling prices, next three months: +45 (Prev: +51)

The NZIER’s quarterly survey of business opinion showed a strong lift in general confidence in
September, despite resurgent fuel prices and the uncertainty around the looming election. Firms were
more confident about their prospects for the next few months, even though their recent performance
remained rough in many industries. 
The survey was held over a relatively compressed period (9 to 28 September), with around 40% of
responses coming back on the first day. Later responses were less confident, as the resurgence in
world oil prices was finally reflected in prices at the pump over the course of the month. 
General business sentiment rose to a net 40% positive compared to a net 14% in June, returning to
around the levels seen in the December quarter last year (before the Middle East conflict). However,
the own-activity measures, which tend to have a closer correspondence with GDP growth, were more
subdued. Trading activity over the last three months was slightly weaker than in June, while
expectations for the quarter ahead saw a more modest four-point increase to +15%. 
Other forward-looking measures were also up for the quarter. Hiring intentions rose strongly to a net
23% positive (although past employment was down). Notably, firms continue to report that workers are
becoming harder to find, despite the ongoing rise in unemployment over the last couple of years. That
said, the proportion of firms citing labour as a factor limiting output declined this quarter. Investment
intentions picked up, more so for plant and machinery than for buildings. 
The results were mixed across industries. Manufacturing and services were more upbeat for the
months ahead but reported substantially weaker performance over the last quarter. In contrast, builders
and merchants reported both stronger past results and greater optimism about the near-term outlook. 
The pricing measures in the survey were down compared to June – not entirely surprising as fuel
prices were lower on average through the September quarter compared to the June quarter. The
notable exception was merchants, who expected both higher costs and prices for the quarter ahead. 
The implications for the RBNZ ahead of its 28 October policy review are unclear – there is certainly no
smoking gun in this report that would demand that the RBNZ abandon the “pause” strategy
communicated at the September MPS. Optimism among businesses remains high despite the obvious
headwinds, but there is still a wide gap between hopes and reality. The pricing measures have eased
compared to last quarter, but that may simply confirm the RBNZ’s expectation that annual inflation will
drop from the 4.1% recorded last quarter as fuel prices eased. The September quarter CPI, released
on 22 October, may be more informative in that respect. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist

## Document 287: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard
Infrastructure spending accounts for the majority of the funding

Chart of the Week / 23 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com
3 ECB staff macroeconomic projections for the euro area, September 2026

地產 中立 升息對REITs喜憂參半，轉注意現金流成長動能。
1
《升息不利地產REITs，但租賃期短者表現較佳》
利率變動會直接牽動房地產投資信託基金 (REITs)的
融資成本增加 ，壓縮可分配現金流與股利 ，依高盛

研究報告顯示，從2006年起的10年期美國國債收益
率上升至少40個基點的滾動六個月週期內，REITs的
表現比標普 500指數低 6.7%，過去五年 ，大多數
REIT 與 10 年期公債殖利率呈負相關，主要因為受
到租賃期間長短的影響 ，其中以飯店業及倉儲設施
因租賃期間較短可當下反應租金變動 ，購物中心因
經濟表現佳 ，可支配所得增加 ，所以表現反而較佳 ；
淨租賃、醫療保健REITs表現最差，但以近期而言，
業績 及營運模式的調整重要性也上升 ，醫療 保健
REITs受個別基本面不同，部分業者營運模式從過去
較長天期的淨租賃轉往養老住宅 ，縮短租賃期，老
年人口財富負擔能力提升，支撐老人住房市場定價
資料來源：高盛，2026/9/18，中國信託商業銀行彙整，
2026/10/1

力，租金增加 ，入住率也提升接近 90%；資料中心
中，雖與超大型業者簽訂長約 ，其他機櫃承租客戶

《預期地產交易價格降溫 ，4Q26交易量增長放緩 》

Green Street CPPI 數據顯示，全美商業地產價格
相較於低點已呈現微幅回升 ，8月上漲0.8%。過去
一年累計上漲5%，但美國國債收益率的上升及對借
貸成本的影響 ，恐促使購房者重新考慮支付意願 ，
預計漲勢即將降溫。依高盛統計截至8月的季度累計
地產交易量年增 18%(扣除合併 )，高於世邦魏理仕
和仲量聯行對 3Q26投資銷售額年增 13%和14%的
預期，8月份的強勁成長主要由工業地產和辦公地產
推動，而多戶住宅市場仍然落後 。工業倉儲主要受
惠供應過剩逐漸下降 ，租賃活動受到美國製造業回
流、近岸外包與第三方物流供應鏈重組支撐 ，優質
近港與門戶型物流中心空置率開始回落；辦公室物
業則呈現科技與專業服務企業偏好承租一線 A級商
辦，而不青睞 B/C級商辦 ，拆除舊型辦公室 、改建
住宅等 。多戶住宅仍在消化陽光帶 (德州、佛州等)
過剩的存量，壓抑短期租金漲幅 。受30年期固定房
貸利率在9月升至7%的高位及去年基期也高的影響 ，
預期4Q26交易量放緩。

《REITs股息吸引力低，轉注意現金流成長動能》
美REITs的回報在利率下降時會擴張估值，但現美國
10年期公債殖利率多 在5%以上，美國REITs平均股
息收益率約3.9%，相對不具投資吸引力 ，對房地產
持中立。然若考量租金現金流增長 ，租金定價力相
對強勢的族群主要為養老住宅 ，同店淨營利年增率
可達20%上下，但估值偏高，建議回檔可留意。

◼ 貨幣政策與殖利率： 聯準會言論放鷹是為重塑
公信力，9月按兵不動仍為較高機率情境，然不
能排除聯準會為了強化政策訊號而進行 「象徵
性升息 」的可能性 。推演美債長天期殖利率
2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落。
◼ 投資建議： 企業獲利和景氣維持成長 ，利率上
揚壓抑股市估值但研判為暫時性 ，高殖利率信
用債在緩升息環境下表現仍穩健 ，惟債務風險
未緩解，利率敏感度高的長債價格波動較大。
熱門議題/資產影響推估 市場焦點
➢ 8月以來全球公債殖利率上升：美國10年期公債利
率上探4.8%，30年期公債殖利率創下 2007年6月
以來新高，日本10年期公債殖利率創下 1996年以
來新高。主要受財政壓力 、地緣不確定性 、貨幣
政策等多重因素帶動。
➢ 推演殖利率 2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：本
行預估聯準會 2026年尚不至採取升息 ，然鷹派言
論將讓高利率環境短期難解 ，有待2027年通膨壓

力緩解，利率方能適度回落。

美國10年期公債殖利率站上4.8%，股市情緒受到影響但未失控
美債殖利率曲線短端因反應通膨擔憂而上升
盤勢回顧
股市情緒受到影響但未失控，信用債普遍收紅
3
• 升息擔憂推升 10年期公債殖利率站上 4.8%，但信用債過去一個月總報酬仍為正值 ：聯準會主席華許在8月底
Jackson Hole央行年會釋出鷹派訊號，市場提高對9月升息的押注，再加上近期中東地緣政治風險又再推動油價
上漲，美國10年期公債殖利率站上4.8%整數關卡，短天期利率主要反映聯準會升息預期升幅高於長天期利率。利
率走升雖對信用債帶來壓力，但利差收斂及息收仍為總報酬帶來貢獻，過去一個月美國非投資級債及新興主權
債仍上漲0.5%，就連長天期公司債亦仍收紅。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控：華許於7月底FOMC會議表示通膨有進展，然8月偏鷹派表述之後股市情緒受到一
定程度影響，不過修正幅度不大，並未出現恐慌情緒。而長天期利率其實在 8月初就開始出現上行趨勢 ，不過
多數指數8月以來仍收正報酬，僅利率敏感性較強的地產和公用事業表現較為落後。

「財政壓力+地緣不確定性+AI支出 」= 高利率環境短期難解
期限溢酬+升息預期共同推升殖利率居高不下
環境分析
資料來源：(左) NY Fed ，2026/9；(右)中國信託商業銀行編繪，2026/6。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/8
• 財政可持續性疑慮 +地緣衝突頻繁，推升風險溢價要求：觀察聯準會經濟學家的 ACM模型可發現 ，期限溢價
(承擔未來利率變動、通膨與供給風險所要求的額外補償報酬 )達80個基點，也是去年以來美債殖利率走勢和降
息趨勢背離的主因。其中，近年地緣政治風險不確定性提高，以及主要國家推動大規模財政政策，使得財政與
債務可持續性疑慮不斷升溫等結構性因素，是市場對持有長端債券要求的風險貼水不斷增加。
• AI資本支出進一步推升中性利率水準：短期間，AI浪潮帶動科技巨頭大幅增加資料中心、AI晶片、電力基礎設
施及雲端設備投資，使企業資本支出需求顯著上升，推升整體資金需求，帶動實質利率走揚。長期而言，隨著
AI帶動生產力的顯著提升，增強了企業投資報酬率與經濟體的潛在成長率，經濟便能承受較高利率水準，促使

市場上修中性利率(r*)預估。

事件說明
通膨已見高峰，然需待2Q27方見明顯下滑
• 華許放鷹，重燃升息預期：聯準會主席華許在7月底FOMC會議表示通膨有進展，8月公布的數據也驗證該看法，
升息預期一度大幅降溫，但8月底JacksonHole他卻展現相當鷹派的論調，9月升息的機率再度回升到50%以上。
• 鴿派論述無法壓制長天期利率，聯準會轉換策略：市場近期呈現一種較特殊的現象，當升息預期下降、短端利
率回落時，長天期公債殖利率卻不降反升，甚至迫使財政部透過公債回購政策來緩和壓力。這可能反映市場擔
憂聯準會過早轉向鴿派，進而削弱其長期抗通膨的公信力，促使部分投資人減持長天期美債。在此背景下，本
行認為華許轉趨鷹派的重要目的之一，是維持市場對聯準會抗通膨決心的信任。若9月公布的8月通膨數據符合預
期，9月FOMC按兵不動仍為較高機率情境。在聯準會維持鷹派但不升息的情境下，本行預估下半年美國10年期
公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-4.8%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在5.1%-5.5%之間。

進入緊縮周期的機率低，短線影響股債行情但不影響趨勢
推演殖利率2H26偏高表現、1H27適度回落
貨幣政策
過往在景氣高峰下利率上揚，本益比多半承壓
• 進入緊縮周期的機率低：雖然不能排除聯準會為了強化政策訊號與維持公信力，而進行「象徵性升息」，也就是
短期的政策微調。但以目前的通膨與就業數據趨勢來看，並不會是新一輪升息循環的起點。通膨壓力在2027上
半年預估將有較明顯的緩解，屆時聯準會的立場將再轉換。
• 聯準會若升息，10年期利率或先上探5%，暫時壓抑股市估值：推演後續路徑，長天期殖利率將呈現2H26偏高
表現，1H27殖利率適度回落的格局。但若聯準會2H26升息，市場第一時間將因擔憂持續性升息的可能，而使
美國10年期公債殖利率先上探 5%。從長週期角度而言，利率與景氣及股市評價走勢亦步亦趨 ，不過當景氣處
於高峰時利率上揚，本益比多半承壓，因為景氣高峰意味企業獲利再成長空間有限，或股價已經被充分評價。
考量AI基礎建設資本支出週期尚未結束，企業獲利仍保持兩位數成長下，利率上揚對估值的壓抑研判為暫時性。

日本債匯承壓，利差交易平倉與干預日圓皆對美債形成干擾風險
外部風險
資料來源：(左)Bloomberg，2026/8；(右)日本央行，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9
• 《日本成長戰略》+史上最大預算案，日債匯市面臨拋售壓力：5月日本政府擬推動規模達370兆日圓的《日本
成長戰略》後，市場對財政可持續性的疑慮快速升溫，5~8月內外資合計賣超20兆日圓日本公債；隨後日本政
府提出2027財年預算達到143兆日圓、年增16%，加上美國持續對日本升息施加壓力，日本利匯率持續承壓。
• 日本如暴力升息或引發利差交易平倉衝擊美債，然債務壓力限制下機率不高：今年美日利差在日本貨幣政策正
常化後罕見擴大，利差交易餘額(日本金融機構轉移海外資產 )較過去兩年明顯增加，若日本升息過快或日圓快
速升值，觸發利差交易平倉至美債部位減碼影響較大。同時，若日圓貶值壓力失控，財務省加大出售美元資產
進行匯市干預，可能增加美債賣壓推升殖利率，加劇金融市場波動。然美國已設立FIMA Repo機制避免日本大

量賣出美債；而日本債務壓力承重、升息速度受限，加以美債利率維持高檔，短期利差交易大量平倉誘因有限。

2016年聯準會升息循環未對信用債帶來嚴重衝擊
投資觀點
資料來源：(左) Bloomberg、BofA，2026/4/7；(右) Bloomberg，2026/9/1 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/1
• 高殖利率信用債在緩升息環境下表現仍穩健：公債利率持續反映升息預期，市場擔憂信用債出現如2022年般重
挫，然而貨幣政策不變仍為目前基本情境，再者，每次升息大環境不同，美國通膨已見觸頂向下、也不像2022
年時嚴峻，就算升息應只是短暫政策調整 ，信用債表現預期較類似2015年緩升息循環：美 10年期公債利率緩
升，經濟成長持穩支撐信用債上漲。預期在企業獲利及景氣成長循環未結束前，非投資級債這類高殖利率資產
在穩健債信支持下波動可望降低，惟債務風險未緩解，利率敏感度高的長債(尤其是25年以上)價格波動仍大。
• 短線利率反彈，雖然基本面支持輪動，但資金高度參與下須保留部位彈性：過去利率自波段低點回升期間，股
市多能同步上漲，反映殖利率上升通常與景氣及企業獲利改善有關。本次多了財政壓力的結構性因素，限縮未

來幾個月的指數空間，但企業獲利穩健成長的基本趨勢不變，資金並未撤出股市，而是轉向加快輪動布局速度，
投資上仍建議降低單一主題集中度，並且適度提高部位彈性，利用市場震盪時布局投資機會。

图⽚
⼯信部表⽰，下⼀步，将加快推进有关标准制修订，进⼀步提升安全基线要求，对重点汽
⻋⽣产企业持续开展监督检查，依法依规严肃处理存在问题的⽣产企业，并向社会公开曝
光，督促汽⻋⽣产企业加强汽⻋产品测试验证，坚决守牢产品安全底线，推动汽⻋产业⾼
质量发展。
摘要：
记者今天从⼯业和信息化部获悉，⼯业和信息化部、公安部、⽣态环境部、市场监管总局四部
⻔组织30 余家重点汽⻋⽣产企业开展座谈，加强汽⻋产品创新设计和研发测试验证管理。
据了解，汽⻋⽣产企业应落实质量提升专项⾏动⼯作要求，坚决抵制⾮理性竞争，加强汽⻋产
品创新设计和研发测试验证管理，守牢产品安全底线。重点包括：
全⾯加强测试验证，增强研发测试验证能⼒，充分开展产品创新设计安全评估和研发测试验
证，及时消除安全隐患。

如实宣传产品创新设计的使⽤边界、限制条件和研发测试验证等情况，不得作虚假、夸⼤或
引⼈误解的宣传，不得使⽤违反道路交通法规的场景来宣传产品功能，不得诱导驾驶⼈忽视
安全驾驶责任等内容。
⼯业和信息化部表⽰，下⼀步，将加快推进有关标准制修订，进⼀步提升安全基线要求，对重
点汽⻋⽣产企业持续开展监督检查，依法依规严肃处理存在问题的⽣产企业，并向社会公开曝
光，督促汽⻋⽣产企业加强汽⻋产品测试验证，坚决守牢产品安全底线，推动汽⻋产业⾼质量
发展。

## Document 524: 央行规范银行间债券市场债券发行行为，禁止发行人认购、变相认购自己发行的债券

Source PDF: all report pdf/央行规范银行间债券市场债券发行行为，禁止发行人认购、变相认购自己发行的债券.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
《规则》禁⽌发⾏⼈认购、变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券，并规定任何⼈不得直接或间接协助
发⾏⼈认购或变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券；禁⽌发⾏⼈⼲预投资者认购决策或以返费等⽅式
⼲扰定价；明确不同债券发⾏⽅式的定价要求；推动发⾏⼈优化承销机构选聘机制和选聘
标准，提⾼债券承销服务质量。
摘要：
为规范银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏⾏为，维护市场秩序，促进债券市场⾼质量发展，中国⼈⺠银
⾏制定了《银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏管理规则》（下称《规则》）， 10 ⽉ 16 ⽇⾯向社会公开征求意
⻅。
《规则》适⽤于境内及境外发⾏⼈在银⾏间债券市场发⾏的，约定按期还本付息的有价证券。
债券定价是债券发⾏的核⼼环节，关系到债券市场能否将⾦融资源精准配置到实体经济，也是
实现债券市场⾼质量发展的重要前提。
《规则》明确，债券发⾏应当遵循市场化、法治化、公平公正、诚实信⽤原则。债券的发⾏价
格、利率及发⾏过程中涉及的佣⾦费⽤等应当以市场化⽅式确定。任何单位、个⼈不得影响债
券公允定价，不得有违反公平竞争、  进⾏利益输送、  谋取不正当利益、  损害投资者权益、破坏

市场秩序的⾏为。
《规则》共⼆⼗⼋条，从发⾏⼈、承销商、投资⼈三个维度强化对债券发⾏⾏为的规范。
⼀是规范发⾏⼈的发⾏定价⾏为。具体来说，《规则》禁⽌发⾏⼈认购、变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债
券，并规定任何⼈不得直接或间接协助发⾏⼈认购或变相认购⾃⼰发⾏的债券；禁⽌发⾏⼈⼲
预投资者认购决策或以返费等⽅式⼲扰定价；明确不同债券发⾏⽅式的定价要求；推动发⾏⼈
优化承销机构选聘机制和选聘标准，提⾼债券承销服务质量。
⼆是规范承销机构的承销⾏为。《规则》禁⽌低价承销，规定承销费不得明显低于⾏业公允⽔
平；禁⽌事先承诺债券投标价格或发⾏价格（利率）  ；规范包销、代投代缴等⾏为；强化承销
机构利益冲突、信息隔离等内控要求，加强信息披露等市场化约束机制等。
三是规范投资者的认购⾏为。《规则》禁⽌投资者虚假投标；不得蓄意⼤量申报并撤销，扰乱债
券发⾏价格（利率）及配售；不得以⼲扰价格为⽬的，投标价格明显低于市场公允价格。  《规
则》对内幕交易、利益输送等⾏为也提出明确禁⽌性要求。
四是规范簿记建档等发⾏流程。《规则》要求簿记场所应当能够全程记录所有询价、调整、定价

决策信息记录，并形成不可篡改的记录，供事后核查。
五是强化对发⾏定价环节的监督管理。  《规则》明确中国⼈⺠银⾏对银⾏间债券市场债券发⾏
进⾏监督管理；建⽴⼀线监测、⾃律管理、⾏政监管和统⼀执法闭环管理机制；明确中国⼈⺠
银⾏及其分⽀机构可以采取责令整改、监管谈话等监管措施，并依法对违法违规⾏为予以⾏政
处罚；加强⾏刑衔接，  对于涉嫌犯罪的⾏为，  移送司法机关依法追究刑事责任。
来源：澎湃新闻

### Page 2

水準を切り上げた。全体としてみれば、原油高によるコスト増を半導体関連の輸出価格上昇
が相殺し、収益率が高まっているとみられる。

・なお日銀短観の調査は、前月からの変化を問うＰＭＩ等と異なり、比較時点を問わず、単刀
直入に現時点における景況感を尋ねる形式である。そのため、回答にあたって自社の収益計
画を基準にしている企業は多いと考えられる。自社計画を超過していれば「良い」「さほど
良くない」「悪い」の３択から「良い」を選択するはずであるから、そうであれば業況判断
ＤＩの改善は業績上方修正の余地と考えることができる。短観とアナリスト予想の方向感が
一致するのはそうした背景があるからではないか。

・次にインフレ関連項目に目を向けると、企業の物価見通し（全規模・全産業、１年後）は、
販売価格見通し（≒自社製品・サービスの価格設定スタンス）が物価見通し（≒日本の物価上
昇率）を上回る傾向が続いた。物価見通しの＋2.6％に対して、販売価格見通しは＋3.5％と
高水準にある。前回調査対比で、双方とも小幅に低下したとはいえ、ここから判断すると今
後も価格転嫁が勢い付くと予想される。こうした「販売価格見通し＞物価見通し」という構
図はコロナ期以前には観察されなかった新たなものであり、値上げによって収益を確保する
企業行動が定着してきたことを窺わせる。ベア継続や最低賃金上昇といった自社の労働コス
ト増加に加え、仕入価格の値上げなど、社外のコスト増を織り込み、３年や５年後の販売価
格計画を引き上げる企業行動が背景にあろう。

## Document 623: 纳斯达克100站上关键支撑位，多空博弈进入决战时刻

Source PDF: all report pdf/纳斯达克100站上关键支撑位，多空博弈进入决战时刻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

经历⼀轮剧烈抛售后，纳斯达克 100 指数在关键趋势⽀撑位和 100 ⽇均线交汇处形成锤形反
转 K 线，多空双⽅在此展开决定性博弈，守住可能触发强⼒轧空⾏情，跌破则市场或将急速
恶化。该指数远期市盈率已重回 2023 年 AI ⾏情启动以来的估值低点。随着仓位持续出清，
进⼊财报季后盈利端或将成为⽀撑估值的关键变量。
摘要：
⾦融市场分析平台 The Market Ear 认为，纳斯达克 100 指数（ NDX ）正处于技术与基本⾯的双重
考验之中。在经历⼀轮剧烈抛售后，指数在关键趋势⽀撑位和 100 ⽇均线交汇处形成锤形反转 K
线，多空双⽅在此展开决定性博弈，若守住，可能触发强⼒轧空⾏情，若跌破，则市场或将急
速恶化。
本周四盘中，半导体⾏业基⾦ VanEck Semiconductor ETF （ SMH ）和内存产业基⾦ Roundhill
Memory ETF （ DRAM ）相关标的成交量暴增⾄近期峰值，多空对冲基⾦账⾯遭受重创，全球系
统性多空策略⼀⽇下跌 1.4% ，接近四个标准差的极端波动。与此同时，科技股信⽤违约互换

（ CDS ）利差持续⾛阔，信⽤市场与股票市场的压⼒信号罕⻅地趋于⼀致。
当前， NDX 远期市盈率已回落⾄约 21 倍，重回 2023 年 AI ⾏情启动以来的估值低点。分析⼈⼠指
出，随着仓位持续出清，进⼊财报季后盈利端或将成为⽀撑估值的关键变量。
关键⽀撑位：锤形 K 线与均线共振
据 The Market Ear 分析， NDX 期货周四在⾃ 4 ⽉低点以来维持的上升趋势线上打出⼀根⼤型锤形
K 线， 100 ⽇均线亦在同⼀区域形成⽀撑共振，令该位置成为当前市场的核⼼战场。
技术层⾯的逻辑清晰：若多⽅能够守住这⼀区域，此前遭受重创的空头仓位将⾯临被迫回补的
压⼒，轧空⾏情的潜在⼒度不容⼩觑；反之，⼀旦⽀撑失守，缺乏有效承接的市场结构将使跌
势迅速加剧。
信⽤与股票：压⼒信号终于同步
此轮科技股下跌的⼀个值得关注的变化，在于信⽤市场与股票市场的信号终于趋于⼀致。此
前，科技股 CDS 利差曾出现⼀次明显跳升，但彼时 NDX 不跌反涨，令市场参与者困惑不解。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⽽本轮调整中，信⽤利差⾛阔与科技股下跌同步发⽣，两个市场 " 终于开始说同⼀种语⾔ " 。科技
股隐含波动率指标 VXN 也随之⾛⾼，逼近 9 ⽉⾼点，尽管从更⻓周期来看，波动率在过去两个⽉
整体仍处于区间震荡，尚未出现趋势性突破。
半导体成交量暴增：⽌损出清还是再度去⻛险？
本周四的抛售过程中，热⻔交易的标的出现异常放量。
当天，半导体⾏业 ETF SMH 和内存业 ETF DRAM 均录得近期最⾼单⽇成交量，随后买盘介⼊，
市场出现阶段性企稳。
⾼盛 One-Delta 交易台负责⼈ Rich Privorotsky 指出，多空账簿正在积极压缩总敞⼝（ de-
grossing ），部分成交量可能反映的是被动⽌损平仓，⽽⾮主动的基本⾯判断转变。全球系统性
多空策略昨⽇下跌 1.4% ，为接近四个标准差的极端单⽇表现，亏损的核⼼已不再是净敞⼝⽅
向，⽽是总敞⼝规模 —— 多头遭遇强⼒轧空的同时，空头也⾯临同等⼒度的逼空，双向杀伤特
征明显。
AI 资本开⽀：需求坚实，但结构性⽭盾未解
Privorotsky 对半导体板块的短期前景持谨慎乐观态度，认为 AI 复合体中杠杆最⾼的部分已从昨

⽇低点出现明显反弹，信⽤层⾯的恐慌情绪在短期内或已过度定价，此处或为阶段性底部。
他提出⼀个潜在的相对价值交易思路：做空超⼤规模云服务商（ hyperscalers ），同时做多半导
体。
然⽽，结构性⽭盾依然存在。 Privorotsky 指出，过多资本正在追逐数量有限、具备电⼒就绪条
件的算⼒基础设施，重定价过程远未结束，可能以渐进⽅式演进，也可能以剧烈错位的形式爆
发，并向更⼴泛市场蔓延。
⼀个值得关注的⽭盾现象是：尽管技术折旧加速， B200 、 H200 、 H100 乃⾄⽼款 A100 的现货算
⼒租赁价格依然保持强劲。这意味着 AI 融资端承压，但实际算⼒供给仍然稀缺，既有基础设施持
有者的竞争优势短期内难以撼动。

### Page 3

收益率下⾏不再是科技股的护⾝符
⾃ 9 ⽉以来， NDX 与美国 10 年期国债收益率呈现出罕⻅的同向联动 —— 科技股与收益率同涨同
跌，打破了 “ 利率下⾏利好⾼估值成⻓股 ” 的传统叙事框架。
这⼀相关性表明，当前主导市场定价的核⼼变量是增⻓预期与⻛险偏好，⽽⾮折现率本⾝。换
⾔之，若收益率因增⻓担忧⽽下⾏，对科技股⽽⾔并⾮利好信号。
市场当前更担⼼的是增⻓放缓，⽽⾮利率⽔平。
估值触底：财报季或成转折催化剂
NDX 远期市盈率已回落⾄约 21 倍，重新触及 2023 年 AI ⾏情启动以来的估值低点。
⾼盛全球银⾏与市场部美国 TMT ⾏业专家 Peter Callahan 指出，这⼀⽔平或已构成四重底的技术
形态，为即将到来的财报季提供了相对有利的估值基础。
在仓位持续出清、情绪趋于极端的背景下，盈利端的实际表现将成为决定市场⽅向的关键。若
企业盈利能够兑现预期，估值修复的空间或将打开；反之，若基本⾯进⼀步恶化，当前技术⽀
撑位的失守将难以避免。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
